{"parentUuid":"90a131d6-f573-4008-9514-fbff071703b4","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ae01ef08a3cfed581","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. Triggers on \"generate an excalidraw image\", \"excalidraw-codex\", \"use codex for excalidraw\", or any request for excalidraw-style diagrams where no engine is named. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Sibling skill [[excalidraw-gemini]] does the same job through the paid Gemini API. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on any mention of LinkedIn, including \"write a LinkedIn post\", \"make a LinkedIn carousel\", \"swipeable LinkedIn post\", \"LinkedIn slides\", \"check my LinkedIn\", \"how did my posts do\", \"LinkedIn competitors\", \"post on LinkedIn\", \"browse LinkedIn feed\". Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Also use it for finding something to post about — \"find me a LinkedIn topic\", \"run a source sweep\", \"scout me something on MCP\", \"what should I post today\", or reviewing the drafts the daily scout left in Slack\n- newsletter-writer: Write personal newsletters in a conversational, essay-style format inspired by Ali Abdaal's style. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. The analysis engine behind the acs/gap-scout routine, but also runs standalone. Use this skill WHENEVER the user pastes or links a coding/AI video (podcast, talk, tutorial, feature walkthrough) and wants the ACS angle: \"what ACS video could come from this\", \"run the gap scout on this\", \"gap-check this against the catalog\", \"find the spine idea\", \"is this already covered in the school\", \"what should I film from this\", \"mine this video for ACS\", or just drops a transcript/link and asks what's video-worthy. Also the per-video worker the gap-scout routine calls for each swept video. Use whenever the user wants to brainstorm titles, generate thumbnails (text or images), record A/B test results, analyze title/thumbnail performance, or asks things like \"suggest titles\", \"what titles should I test\", \"make a thumbnail\", \"thumbnail variations\", \"5 thumbnails for this video\", \"Matt style\", \"Nate style\", \"record these results\", \"what's working\", or \"update the A/B test results\". Also trigger when the user shares a screenshot of YouTube A/B test results and wants analysis or recording. Use when the user asks to \"create a diagram\", \"visualize the flow\", \"draw a sequence diagram\", \"show me how X works\", or needs visual documentation of system interactions, workflows, database schemas, or technical processes.\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; 0 : rounded;\n}\n\n/**\n * Round a numeric value up to the next tenth, ensuring non-negative output.\n */\nexport function roundUpToTenth(value: number): number {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) {\n    return 0;\n  }\n\n  const rounded = Math.ceil((value - Number.EPSILON) * 10) / 10;\n  return rounded <= 0 ? 0 : rounded;\n}\n\n/**\n * Retry configuration options\n */\nexport interface RetryOptions {\n  /** Maximum number of retry attempts (default: 3) */\n  maxRetries?: number;\n  /** Initial delay in milliseconds (default: 1000ms) */\n  initialDelayMs?: number;\n  /** Backoff multiplier for exponential delay (default: 2) */\n  backoffMultiplier?: number;\n  /** Optional callback invoked before each retry attempt */\n  onRetry?: (attempt: number, error: Error, delayMs: number) => void;\n}\n\n/**\n * Retry an async operation with exponential backoff\n */\nexport async function retryWithBackoff<T>(\n  fn: () => Promise<T>,\n  options: RetryOptions = {}\n): Promise<T> {\n  const {\n    maxRetries = 3,\n    initialDelayMs = 1000,\n    backoffMultiplier = 2,\n    onRetry,\n  } = options;\n\n  let lastError: Error;\n\n  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= maxRetries; attempt++) {\n    try {\n      return await fn();\n    } catch (error) {\n      lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));\n\n      if (attempt === maxRetries) {\n        throw lastError;\n      }\n\n      const delayMs = initialDelayMs * Math.pow(backoffMultiplier, attempt);\n\n      if (onRetry) {\n        onRetry(attempt + 1, lastError, delayMs);\n      }\n\n      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, delayMs));\n    }\n  }\n\n  throw lastError!;\n}\n","numLines":98,"startLine":1,"totalLines":98}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3cbc69c8-aebd-4671-8542-ebe46dc895e3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"e1f979f1-cfc5-4520-9a22-4d30fae80f32","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"216b0b4f-2c78-49fd-ba56-566f7fa59541","agentId":"ae01ef08a3cfed581","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SX6LWDWKYLoFiAujQ3PdJL","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t// MICROSOFT MAI-TRANSCRIBE 1.5 PROVIDER (Azure Speech / Foundry)\n2\t// Multilingual transcription model — 43 languages, phrase-list biasing, $0.006/min.\n3\t//\n4\t// API: POST https://<resource>.cognitiveservices.azure.com/speechtotext/transcriptions:transcribe?api-version=2025-10-15\n5\t// Auth: Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key\n6\t//\n7\t// Request shape: multipart/form-data with two parts\n8\t//   - `audio`: the binary audio with the original Content-Type\n9\t//   - `definition`: JSON describing the enhanced model + locales + phraseList\n10\t//\n11\t// IMPORTANT: the `definition` part MUST be sent as application/json. The\n12\t// service rejects text/plain JSON with HTTP 400.\n13\t\n14\timport { AZURE_MAI_MAX_BYTES } from '../lib/constants';\n15\timport { computeAzureMaiTranscriptionCost } from '../lib/cost-calculator';\n16\timport { AudioTooLargeError, ProviderUnavailableError, UnsupportedAudioFormatError } from './types';\n17\timport type { ProviderRequestContext, TranscriptionResult } from './types';\n18\timport { computeUploadTimeoutMs, fetchWithTimeout, logProviderEvent, readErrorBodyPreview } from './utils';\n19\t\n20\tconst MAX_PHRASES = 100;\n21\tconst MAX_PHRASE_LEN = 50;\n22\t// MAI-Transcribe 1.5 documents only WAV, MP3, and FLAC as accepted upload\n23\t// formats. Anything else (m4a, mp4, webm, opus, ogg, aac, wma) is rejected\n24\t// upstream with a generic error; we surface a typed 415 instead so the\n25\t// client can re-encode.\n26\t// Ref: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/mai-transcribe\n27\tconst AZURE_MAI_ACCEPTED_FORMATS = ['wav', 'mp3', 'flac'] as const;\n28\t\n29\tfunction getExtension(contentType: string): 'wav' | 'mp3' | 'flac' {\n30\t  const lower = contentType.toLowerCase();\n31\t  if (lower.includes('wav')) return 'wav';\n32\t  if (lower.includes('mp3') || lower.includes('mpeg')) return 'mp3';\n33\t  if (lower.includes('flac')) return 'flac';\n34\t  throw new UnsupportedAudioFormatError('Azure MAI', contentType, AZURE_MAI_ACCEPTED_FORMATS);\n35\t}\n36\t\n37\tfunction parsePhraseList(initialPrompt: string): string[] {\n38\t  return initialPrompt\n39\t    .split(/[,\\n;]+/)\n40\t    .map(t => t.trim().replace(/^[-*]\\s*/, ''))\n41\t    .filter(t => t.length > 0 && t.length <= MAX_PHRASE_LEN)\n42\t    .slice(0, MAX_PHRASES);\n43\t}\n44\t\n45\tfunction normalizeLocale(language: string): string {\n46\t  // MAI-Transcribe 1.5 wants two-letter language codes (`en`, `ja`, `fr`).\n47\t  // The wider Fast-Transcription API accepts BCP-47 (`en-US`) but the MAI\n48\t  // doc explicitly uses 2-letter; strip the region subtag so we match docs\n49\t  // and stay forward-compatible if Azure tightens validation.\n50\t  return language.toLowerCase().split('-')[0];\n51\t}\n52\t\n53\t// MAI-Transcribe 1.5 is available in 4 Azure regions: eastus, westus,\n54\t// northeurope, southeastasia. 'eastus';\n  }\n  return preferred;\n}\n\nfunction getRegionKey(region: AzureMaiRegion): string | undefined {\n  switch (region) {\n    case 'eastus': return process.env.AZURE_SPEECH_KEY_EASTUS;\n    case 'northeurope': return process.env.AZURE_SPEECH_KEY_NORTHEUROPE;\n    case 'southeastasia': return process.env.AZURE_SPEECH_KEY_SOUTHEASTASIA;\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function transcribeWithAzureMai(\n  audio: ArrayBuffer,\n  contentType: string,\n  language?: string,\n  initialPrompt?: string,\n  context: ProviderRequestContext = {},\n): Promise<TranscriptionResult> {\n 'flac';\n  try {\n    ext = getExtension(contentType);\n  } catch (error) {\n    if (error instanceof UnsupportedAudioFormatError) {\n      logProviderEvent(provider, 'unsupported_audio_format', {\n        contentType,\n        acceptedFormats: AZURE_MAI_ACCEPTED_FORMATS,\n      }, context);\n    }\n    throw error;\n  }\n\n  const azureRegion = pickAzureRegion();\n  const apiKey = getRegionKey(azureRegion);\n  if (!apiKey) {\n    throw new Error(`AZURE_SPEECH_KEY_${azureRegion.toUpperCase()} not configured`);\n  }\n\n  const url = `https://${azureRegion}.api.cognitive.microsoft.com/speechtotext/transcriptions:transcribe?api-version=2025-10-15`;\n\n  const isMonolingual = language && language.toLowerCase() !== 'auto';\n  const phrases = initialPrompt ? parsePhraseList(initialPrompt) : [];\n\n  const definition: Record<string, unknown> = {\n    enhancedMode: {\n      enabled: true,\n      model: 'mai-transcribe-1.5',\n    },\n  };\n  if (isMonolingual) {\n    definition.locales = [normalizeLocale(language!)];\n  }\n  if (phrases.length > 0) {\n    definition.phraseList = { phrases };\n  }\n\n  const form = new Some original\n  // browser MIME types (e.g. `audio/webm; codecs=opus`) have tripped server-\n  // side validation in the past — octet-stream + filename is the safe shape.\n  form.append('audio', new Blob([audio], { type: 'application/octet-stream' }), `audio.${ext}`);\n  // `definition` MUST land as application/json — Azure rejects text/plain.\n  form.append(\n    'definition',\n    new Blob([JSON.stringify(definition)], { type: 'application/json' }),\n    'definition.json',\n  );\n\n  logProviderEvent(provider, 'prepare', {\n    audioBytes: audio.byteLength,\n    contentType,\n    language: language || 'auto',\n    phraseCount: phrases.length,\n    azureRegion,\n    flyRegion: process.env.FLY_REGION,\n  }, context);\n\n  const response = await fetchWithTimeout(provider, url, {\n    method: 'POST',\n    headers: {\n      'Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key': apiKey,\n    },\n    body: form,\n  }, context, computeUploadTimeoutMs(audio.byteLength));\n\n  if (!response.ok) {\n    const errorText = await readErrorBodyPreview(response);\n    const elapsedMs = Math.round(performance.now() - startedAt);\n    const kind = response.status >= 500 ? 'rate_limit' : 'http_error';\n\n    logProviderEvent(provider, 'http_error', {\n      elapsedMs,\n      status: response.status,\n      kind,\n      bodyPreview: errorText,\n    }, context);\n\n    if (response.status === 401) {\n      throw new Error('Azure Speech subscription key is invalid or expired');\n    }\n    if (response.status === 403) {\n      // 403 from Azure Speech is usually \"subscription suspended\" or\n      // \"quota exceeded for free tier\", not \"wrong key\".\n      throw new Error('Azure Speech subscription is disabled or out of quota');\n    }\n    if (response.status === 402) {\n      throw new Error('Azure Speech account has insufficient funds');\n    }\n    if (response.status === 429) {\n      throw new ProviderUnavailableError('Azure MAI', 'rate limit exceeded');\n    }\n    if (response.status >= 500) {\n      throw new ProviderUnavailableError('Azure MAI', `upstream 5xx: ${response.status}`);\n    }\n\n    throw new Error(`Azure MAI error: ${response.status}`);\n  }\n\n  const data = await response.json() as {\n    combinedPhrases?: Array<{ text?: string }>;\n    durationMilliseconds?: number;\n    phrases?: Array<{ locale?: string }>;\n  };\n\n  const transcript = data.combinedPhrases?.[0]?.text || '';\n  const durationSeconds = (data.durationMilliseconds || 0) / 1000;\n  const detectedLanguage = data.phrases?.[0]?.locale;\n\n  if (!transcript || transcript.trim().length === 0) {\n    logProviderEvent(provider, 'no_speech', {\n      elapsedMs: Math.round(performance.now() - '../providers/mistral-llm';\n\nexport type LLMProvider = 'cerebras' | 'groq' | 'anthropic' | 'grok' | 'openai' | 'gemini' | 'mistral';\n\nexport const DEFAULT_LLM_PROVIDER: LLMProvider = 'cerebras';\n\nexport const LLM_PROVIDER_NAMES: Record<LLMProvider, string> = {\n  cerebras: 'cerebras-gpt-oss-120b',\n  groq: 'groq-gpt-oss-120b',\n  anthropic: 'claude-haiku-4-5',\n  grok: 'xai-grok-4.3',\n  openai: 'openai-gpt-5-mini',\n  gemini: 'gemini-2.5-flash',\n  mistral: 'mistral-small-latest',\n};\n\n// Served name per (provider, resolved-model) pair, for the X-LLM-Provider\n// response header / log. The default model maps back to LLM_PROVIDER_NAMES so\n// single-model providers (and the default of the multi-model ones) are\n// unchanged; the non-default allowlisted models of the multi-model providers\n// (openai/gemini/mistral) get their own label so the response reflects the model\n// actually used instead of the provider default. MUST stay in sync with\n// LLM_PROVIDER_MODELS allowlists.\nconst LLM_SERVED_NAMES: Partial<Record<LLMProvider, Record<string, string>>> = {\n  openai: { 'gpt-5-mini': 'openai-gpt-5-mini', 'gpt-5-nano': 'openai-gpt-5-nano' },\n  gemini: { 'gemini-2.5-flash': 'gemini-2.5-flash', 'gemini-2.5-flash-lite': 'gemini-2.5-flash-lite' },\n  mistral: { 'mistral-small-latest': 'mistral-small-latest', 'open-mistral-nemo': 'open-mistral-nemo' },\n};\n\n/**\n * Served name for the X-LLM-Provider response header / log: the (provider,\n * model) pair the request was actually answered with. For the single-model\n * providers (and any model not in the served-name map) this is the static\n * LLM_PROVIDER_NAMES label; for the multi-model providers' non-default models it\n * echoes the resolved model so callers (and the smoke test) can tell a\n * non-default model apart from the provider default.\n */\nexport function servedLLMName(provider: LLMProvider, model: string): string {\n  return LLM_SERVED_NAMES[provider]?.[model] ?? LLM_PROVIDER_NAMES[provider];\n}\n\nconst LLM_PROVIDER_FALLBACKS: Record<LLMProvider, LLMProvider> = {\n  anthropic: 'cerebras',\n  cerebras: 'groq',\n  groq: 'cerebras',\n  grok: 'anthropic',\n  openai: 'anthropic',\n  gemini: 'cerebras',\n  mistral: 'groq',\n};\n\n// Per-provider allowlist of valid X-LLM-Model ids, with the default first. The\n// resolved model is threaded through callWithRetry to the openai/gemini/mistral\n// clients (the 4 single-model providers ignore it). MUST match the model ids in\n// shared-app-classification/cloud-pp-catalog.json.\nconst LLM_PROVIDER_MODELS: Record<LLMProvider, { default: string; allowed: readonly string[] }> = {\n  cerebras: { default: 'gpt-oss-120b', allowed: ['gpt-oss-120b'] },\n  groq: { default: 'openai/gpt-oss-120b', allowed: ['openai/gpt-oss-120b'] },\n  anthropic: { default: 'claude-haiku-4-5', allowed: ['claude-haiku-4-5'] },\n  grok: { default: 'grok-4.3', allowed: ['grok-4.3'] },\n  openai: { default: 'gpt-5-mini', allowed: ['gpt-5-mini', 'gpt-5-nano'] },\n  gemini: { default: 'gemini-2.5-flash', allowed: ['gemini-2.5-flash', 'gemini-2.5-flash-lite'] },\n  mistral: { default: 'mistral-small-latest', allowed: ['mistral-small-latest', 'open-mistral-nemo'] },\n};\n\nexport function defaultModelFor(provider: LLMProvider): string {\n  return LLM_PROVIDER_MODELS[provider].default;\n}\n\nexport function fallbackProviderFor(provider: LLMProvider): LLMProvider {\n  return LLM_PROVIDER_FALLBACKS[provider];\n}\n\n/**\n * Extract LLM provider from X-LLM-Provider header.\n * Returns default provider if header is missing or invalid.\n */\nexport function extractLLMProvider(request: Request): LLMProvider {\n  const header = request.headers.get('x-llm-provider')?.toLowerCase().trim();\n\n  switch (header) {\n    case 'groq':\n    case 'cerebras':\n    case 'anthropic':\n    case 'grok':\n    case 'openai':\n    case 'gemini':\n    case 'mistral':\n      return header;\n    default:\n      return DEFAULT_LLM_PROVIDER;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Resolve the model for a provider from the X-LLM-Model header, validating it\n * against the provider's allowlist. Missing or invalid models fall back to the\n * provider default so a bad header never bills the wrong (or no) model.\n */\nexport function resolveLLMModel(provider: LLMProvider, request: Request): string {\n  const requested = request.headers.get('x-llm-model')?.toLowerCase().trim();\n  const config = LLM_PROVIDER_MODELS[provider];\n  if (requested && config.allowed.includes(requested)) {\n    return requested;\n  }\n  return config.default;\n}\n\n/**\n * Retry LLM call with exponential backoff. `model` is the resolved (allowlisted)\n * model id — the multi-model providers (openai/gemini/mistral) route on it; the\n * single-model providers ignore it.\n */\nexport async function callWithRetry(\n  provider: LLMProvider,\n  payload: CorrectionRequestPayload,\n  requestId: string,\n  maxRetries: number,\n  model: string\n): Promise<Awaited<ReturnType<typeof requestCerebrasChat>>> {\n  return retryWithBackoff(\n    () => {\n      if (provider === 'anthropic') return requestAnthropicChat(payload, requestId);\n      if (provider === 'grok') return requestXaiGrokChat(payload, requestId);\n      if (provider === 'openai') return requestOpenAIChat(payload, requestId, model);\n      if (provider === 'gemini') return requestGeminiChat(payload, requestId, model);\n      if (provider === 'mistral') return requestMistralChat(payload, requestId, model);\n      return provider === 'cerebras'\n        ? requestCerebrasChat(payload, requestId)\n        : requestGroqChat(payload, requestId);\n    },\n    {\n      maxRetries,\n      initialDelayMs: 1000,\n      backoffMultiplier: 2,\n      onRetry: (attempt, error, delayMs) => {\n        console.warn(`[llm] ${provider} failed - retrying`, {\n          attempt,\n          error: error.message,\n          delayMs,\n        });\n      },\n    }\n  );\n}\n\nfunction getErrorStatus(error: unknown): number | undefined {\n  if (!error || typeof error !== 'object') {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n\n  const status = (error as { status?: unknown }).status;\n  if (typeof status === 'number') {\n    return status;\n  }\n\n  const message = (error as { message?: unknown }).message;\n  if (typeof message === 'string') {\n    const match = message.match(/status\\s+(\\d{3})/i);\n    if (match) {\n      return Number(match[1]);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/**\n * Check if an error should trigger provider fallback (5xx).\n */\nexport function shouldFallback(error: unknown): boolean {\n  const status = getErrorStatus(error);\n  return typeof status === 'number' && status >= 500 && status <= 599;\n}\n","numLines":185,"startLine":1,"totalLines":185}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a2e146fe-fac7-47f8-9c96-762b25b13cc8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_attempt_start', { provider, attempt: 1 });\n\n  try {\n    const primaryRetries = retriesFor(provider);\n    llmResponse = await callWithRetry(provider, payload, requestId, primaryRetries, model);\n  } catch (error) {\n    logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_attempt_fail', {\n      provider,\n      attempt: 1,\n      error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),\n    });\n\n    if (shouldFallback(error)) {\n      providerUsed = fallbackProviderFor(provider);\n      modelUsed = defaultModelFor(providerUsed);\n      const fallbackRetries = retriesFor(providerUsed);\n\n      logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_fallback_start', { requestedProvider: provider, provider: providerUsed });\n\n      try {\n        llmResponse = await callWithRetry(providerUsed, payload, requestId, fallbackRetries, modelUsed);\n      } catch (fallbackError) {\n        logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.request_fail', {\n          reason: 'llm_fallback_failed',\n          provider: providerUsed,\n          error: fallbackError instanceof Error ? { requestId });\n      }\n    } else {\n      logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.request_fail', {\n        reason: 'llm_failed_no_fallback',\n        provider,\n        error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),\n      });\n      return errorResponse(500, 'Post-processing failed', error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error), { requestId });\n    }\n  }\n\n  logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_attempt_done', {\n    provider: providerUsed,\n    outputChars: rawResponseLength(llmResponse.raw),\n    costUsd: llmResponse.costUsd,\n  });\n\n  let correctedText: string;\n  let costUsd = llmResponse.costUsd;\n\n  try {\n    const evaluation = evaluateCompletionResponse(llmResponse.raw, text);\n\n    if (evaluation.accepted) {\n      correctedText = evaluation.text;\n    } else if (evaluation.failure === 'prompt_leakage') {\n      // Recomputed only for the outputChars metric below — evaluateCompletionResponse\n      // already extracted this successfully, so this call cannot throw.\n      const leakedText = stripCleanMarkers(extractCorrectedText(llmResponse.raw));\n      logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.prompt_leakage_detected', {\n        provider: providerUsed,\n        inputChars: text.length,\n        outputChars: leakedText.length,\n      });\n\n      const alternateProvider: LLMProvider = fallbackProviderFor(providerUsed);\n      const alternateRetries = retriesFor(alternateProvider);\n\n      logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_leakage_retry_start', { requestedProvider: providerUsed, provider: alternateProvider });\n\n      try {\n        const alternateModel = defaultModelFor(alternateProvider);\n        const retryResponse = await callWithRetry(alternateProvider, payload, requestId, alternateRetries, alternateModel);\n\n        // Bill the retry the moment it succeeds, before any evaluation step\n        // below can throw — alternateModel;\n        costUsd += retryResponse.costUsd;\n\n        const retryEvaluation = evaluateCompletionResponse(retryResponse.raw, text);\n\n        if (retryEvaluation.accepted) {\n          correctedText = retryEvaluation.text;\n        } else if (retryEvaluation.failure === 'prompt_leakage') {\n          logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.prompt_leakage_persisted', {\n 'empty_cleaned_text' : retryEvaluation.reason,\n            fallbackToRaw: true,\n          });\n          correctedText = text;\n        }\n      } catch (retryError) {\n        logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_leakage_retry_fail', {\n          provider: alternateProvider,\n          error: retryError instanceof Error ? retryError.message : String(retryError),\n          fallbackToRaw: true,\n        });\n        correctedText = text;\n      }\n    } else {\n      // output_limit, incomplete_response, or empty_cleaned_text: keep the\n      // raw transcript without spending on an alternate-provider retry.\n      logEvent(requestId, startTime, 'post_process.llm_incomplete', {\n        provider: providerUsed,\n        state: evaluation.state,\n        reason: evaluation.failure === 'empty_cleaned_text' ? Verifies the license status is \"granted\" (not revoked/disabled)\n121\t */\n122\texport async function checkLicenseKey(\n123\t  licenseKey: string\n124\t): Promise<LicenseCheckResult> {\n125\t  // Query database for the license\n126\t  let license = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey.trim());\n127\t\n128\t  // POLAR FALLBACK: If not found in DB, try Polar API\n129\t  if (!license) {\n130\t    const polarImport = await importLicenseFromPolar(licenseKey.trim());\n131\t\n132\t    if (!polarImport.success) {\n133\t      return {\n134\t        valid: false,\n135\t        error: polarImport.error || \"License key not found\",\n136\t        status: 400,\n137\t      };\n138\t    }\n139\t\n140\t    // Re-query the newly imported license\n141\t    license = await findAccountById(polarImport.licenseId!);\n142\t\n143\t    if (!license) {\n144\t      return {\n145\t        valid: false,\n146\t        error: \"Failed to retrieve imported license\",\n147\t        status: 500,\n148\t      };\n149\t    }\n150\t  }\n151\t\n152\t  // Check status\n153\t  if (license.status !== \"granted\") {\n154\t    return {\n155\t      valid: false,\n156\t      error: `License is ${license.status}`,\n157\t      status: 400,\n158\t    };\n159\t  }\n160\t\n161\t  return { valid: true, license };\n162\t}\n163\t"}]},"uuid":"938981f4-308f-4266-8d97-f6c2338d0f82","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:33:34.480Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/src/lib/license-validation.ts","content":"        sourceId: polarResult.id,\n      });\n    } catch (creditError) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to create credit balance:\", creditError);\n      // License was created, but credits failed - still return success\n    }\n\n    console.log(\n      `Imported license from Polar: ${licenseKey} for ${email} with 5000 credits`\n    );\n\n    return { success: true, licenseId: license.id };\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(\"Polar license import error:\", err);\n    return {\n      success: false,\n      error: \"Failed to validate with Polar\",\n    };\n  }\n}\n\nexport type LicenseCheckResult =\n  | { valid: true; license: AccountKeyRow }\n  | { valid: false; error: string; status: number };\n\n/**\n * Checks whether a license key is valid (exists and is \"granted\").\n *\n * 1. Looks up the key in the database\n * 2. POLAR FALLBACK: if not found, validates against Polar and imports it\n * 3. Verifies the license status is \"granted\" (not revoked/disabled)\n */\nexport async function checkLicenseKey(\n  licenseKey: string\n): Promise<LicenseCheckResult> {\n  // Query database for the license\n  let license = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey.trim());\n\n  // POLAR FALLBACK: If not found in DB, try Polar API\n  if (!license) {\n    const polarImport = await importLicenseFromPolar(licenseKey.trim());\n\n    if (!polarImport.success) {\n      return {\n        valid: false,\n        error: polarImport.error || \"License key not found\",\n        status: 400,\n      };\n    }\n\n    // Re-query the newly imported license\n    license = await findAccountById(polarImport.licenseId!);\n\n    if (!license) {\n      return {\n        valid: false,\n        error: \"Failed to retrieve imported license\",\n        status: 500,\n      };\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Check status\n  if (license.status !== \"granted\") {\n    return {\n      valid: false,\n      error: `License is ${license.status}`,\n      status: 400,\n    };\n  }\n\n  return { valid: true, license };\n}\n","numLines":74,"startLine":90,"totalLines":163}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4c03b99f-79d9-4ef9-8d25-59bceb952045","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Validate with Polar API\n33\t    const polarResult = await polarClient.customerPortal.licenseKeys.validate({\n34\t      key: licenseKey,\n35\t      organizationId: POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID,\n36\t    });\n37\t\n38\t    // 2. Check if valid\n39\t    if (polarResult.status !== \"granted\") {\n40\t      return { success: false, error: `License is ${polarResult.status}` };\n41\t    }\n42\t\n43\t    // 2b. Dedupe by the stable Polar license-key id (casing-independent).\n44\t    // findAccountByKey is a case-sensitive exact match, so a different casing\n45\t    // or whitespace variant of an already-imported key misses the DB lookup\n46\t    // and reaches this fallback. Without this guard each variant would insert a\n47\t    // fresh license row and grant another 5000 credits. polarResult.id is the\n48\t    // canonical resource id regardless of how the input key was cased.\n49\t    const alreadyImported = await findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId(polarResult.id);\n50\t    if (alreadyImported) {\n51\t      return { success: true, licenseId: alreadyImported.id };\n52\t    }\n53\t\n54\t    // 3. Get customer email - required for import\n55\t    const email = polarResult.customer?.email;\n56\t    if (!email) {\n57\t      return { success: false, error: \"License has no associated email\" };\n58\t    }\n59\t\n60\t    // 4. Get or create user\n61\t    const user = await getOrCreateUser(email, {\n62\t      name: polarResult.customer?.name ?? undefined,\n63\t      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? undefined,\n64\t    });\n65\t    if (!user) {\n66\t      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to get or create user\" };\n67\t    }\n68\t\n69\t    // 5. Insert license into database\n70\t    const license = await insertAccountKey({\n71\t      key: licenseKey,\n72\t      email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\n73\t      userId: user.id,\n74\t      polarLicenseKeyId: polarResult.id,\n75\t      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? null,\n76\t      status: \"granted\",\n77\t    });\n78\t\n79\t    if (!license) {\n80\t      console.error(\"Failed to insert license from Polar\");\n81\t      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to import license\" };\n82\t    }\n83\t\n84\t    // 6. Create credit balance with 5000 credits\n85\t    try {\n86\t      await grantCreditLot({\n87\t        userId: license.userId,\n88\t        amount: 5000,\n89\t        sourceType: \"polar_bundle\",\n90\t        sourceId: polarResult.id,"}]},"uuid":"96227495-0a19-4e8a-8208-d0bc99e8a8b6","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:33:42.641Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/src/lib/license-validation.ts","content":"import { polarClient, POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID } from \"@/lib/clients/polar\";\nimport {\n  findAccountByKey,\n  findAccountById,\n  findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId,\n  insertAccountKey,\n  grantCreditLot,\n  getOrCreateUser,\n  type AccountKeyRow,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Shared license key validation logic.\n *\n * Used by both /api/license/validate and /api/license/activate so that\n * every entry point performs a real database check (with Polar fallback)\n * instead of trusting the key blindly.\n */\n\n/**\n * Import a license from Polar into the local database.\n *\n * Called when a license key is not found in the database but may exist in Polar.\n * Creates the license record, user (if needed), and grants 5000 credits.\n */\nasync function importLicenseFromPolar(licenseKey: string): Promise<{\n  success: boolean;\n  licenseId?: string;\n  error?: string;\n}> {\n  try {\n    // 1. Validate with Polar API\n    const polarResult = await polarClient.customerPortal.licenseKeys.validate({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      organizationId: POLAR_ORGANIZATION_ID,\n    });\n\n    // 2. Check if valid\n    if (polarResult.status !== \"granted\") {\n      return { success: false, error: `License is ${polarResult.status}` };\n    }\n\n    // 2b. Dedupe by the stable Polar license-key id (casing-independent).\n    // findAccountByKey is a case-sensitive exact match, so a different casing\n    // or whitespace variant of an already-imported key misses the DB lookup\n    // and reaches this fallback. Without this guard each variant would insert a\n    // fresh license row and grant another 5000 credits. polarResult.id is the\n    // canonical resource id regardless of how the input key was cased.\n    const alreadyImported = await findAccountByPolarLicenseKeyId(polarResult.id);\n    if (alreadyImported) {\n      return { success: true, licenseId: alreadyImported.id };\n    }\n\n    // 3. Get customer email - required for import\n    const email = polarResult.customer?.email;\n    if (!email) {\n      return { success: false, error: \"License has no associated email\" };\n    }\n\n    // 4. Get or create user\n    const user = await getOrCreateUser(email, {\n      name: polarResult.customer?.name ?? undefined,\n      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? undefined,\n    });\n    if (!user) {\n      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to get or create user\" };\n    }\n\n    // 5. Insert license into database\n    const license = await insertAccountKey({\n      key: licenseKey,\n      email: email.toLowerCase().trim(),\n      userId: user.id,\n      polarLicenseKeyId: polarResult.id,\n      polarCustomerId: polarResult.customerId ?? null,\n      status: \"granted\",\n    });\n\n    if (!license) {\n      console.error(\"Failed to insert license from Polar\");\n      return { success: false, error: \"Failed to import license\" };\n    }\n\n    // 6. Do not add markdown headers, labels, or text outside those markers.';\n13\t\n14\texport interface AnthropicContentBlock {\n15\t  type: 'text' | 'image';\n16\t  text?: string;\n17\t  source?: {\n18\t    type: 'base64';\n19\t    media_type: string;\n20\t    data: string;\n21\t  };\n22\t}\n23\t\n24\texport interface AnthropicMessage {\n25\t  role: 'user' | 'assistant';\n26\t  content: string | AnthropicContentBlock[];\n27\t}\n28\t\n29\texport interface AnthropicStreamResult {\n30\t  stream: ReadableStream<Uint8Array>;\n31\t  costPromise: Promise<number>;\n32\t}\n33\t\n34\t/**\n35\t * Calls the Anthropic Messages API without streaming.\n36\t * Used for post-processing text correction via the /post-process endpoint.\n37\t * Returns shape matching Cerebras/Groq: { raw, usage, costUsd }\n38\t */\n39\texport async function requestAnthropicChat(\n40\t  payload: CorrectionRequestPayload,\n41\t  requestId: string\n42\t): Promise<{ raw: unknown; usage?: GroqUsage; costUsd: number }> {\n43\t  const apiKey = process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY;\n44\t  if (!apiKey) {\n45\t    throw new Error('ANTHROPIC_API_KEY not configured');\n46\t  }\n47\t\n48\t  // Convert OpenAI chat format to Anthropic format:\n49\t  // messages[0] = system prompt, messages[1] = user message\n50\t  const systemContent = (payload.messages[0]?.content || '')\n51\t    + `\\n\\n${ANTHROPIC_WRAPPER_INSTRUCTION}`;\n52\t  const userContent = payload.messages[1]?.content || '';\n53\t\n54\t  const response = await fetch(ANTHROPIC_API_URL, {\n55\t    method: 'POST',\n56\t    headers: {\n57\t      'x-api-key': apiKey,\n58\t      'anthropic-version': ANTHROPIC_VERSION,\n59\t      'content-type': 'application/json',\n60\t    },\n61\t    body: JSON.stringify({\n62\t      model: ANTHROPIC_MODEL,\n63\t      max_tokens: ANTHROPIC_MAX_TOKENS,\n64\t      system: systemContent,\n65\t      messages: [{ role: 'user', content: userContent }],\n66\t      stream: false,\n67\t    }),\n68\t  });\n69\t\n70\t  if (!response.ok) {\n71\t    const errorText = await response.text().catch(() => '');\n72\t    const error = new Error(`Anthropic API error: ${response.status} ${errorText.slice(0, 500)}`);\n73\t    (error as any).status = response.status;\n74\t    throw error;\n75\t  }\n76\t\n77\t  const data = await response.json() as {\n78\t    content: Array<{ type: string; text?: string }>;\n79\t    stop_reason?: string | null;\n80\t    usage: {\n81\t      input_tokens: number;\n82\t      output_tokens: number;\n83\t      cache_creation_input_tokens?: number;\n84\t      cache_read_input_tokens?: number;\n85\t    };\n86\t  };\n87\t\n88\t  const inputTokens = data.usage?.input_tokens || 0;\n89\t  const outputTokens = data.usage?.output_tokens || 0;\n90\t  const cacheCreationTokens = data.usage?.cache_creation_input_tokens || 0;\n91\t  const cacheReadTokens = data.usage?.cache_read_input_tokens || 0;\n92\t  const costUsd = computeAnthropicCost(inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheCreationTokens, cacheReadTokens);\n93\t\n94\t  console.log(`[${requestId}] Anthropic usage: input=${inputTokens}, output=${outputTokens}, cacheWrite=${cacheCreationTokens}, cacheRead=${cacheReadTokens}, cost=$${costUsd.toFixed(6)}`);\n95\t"}]},"uuid":"489ec3dd-b200-4bd3-af57-60178f90615d","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:35:25.676Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/hyperwhisper-cloud/src/providers/anthropic.ts","content":"// ANTHROPIC VISION LLM CLIENT (MESSAGES API, STREAMING)\n// Used by the /assistant endpoint for screen-aware AI responses.\n\nimport { computeAnthropicCost, type GroqUsage } from '../lib/cost-calculator';\nimport { ANTHROPIC_MAX_TOKENS } from '../lib/llm-token-limits';\nimport type { CorrectionRequestPayload } from './groq-llm';\n\nconst ANTHROPIC_API_URL = 'https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages';\nconst ANTHROPIC_MODEL = 'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001';\nconst ANTHROPIC_VERSION = '2023-06-01';\nexport const ANTHROPIC_WRAPPER_INSTRUCTION =\n  'IMPORTANT: Output ONLY the corrected text surrounded by <<CLEANED>> and <<END>> exactly. Do not add markdown headers, labels, or text outside those markers.';\n\nexport interface AnthropicContentBlock {\n  type: 'text' | 'image';\n  text?: string;\n  source?: {\n    type: 'base64';\n    media_type: string;\n    data: string;\n  };\n}\n\nexport interface AnthropicMessage {\n  role: 'user' | 'assistant';\n  content: string | AnthropicContentBlock[];\n}\n\nexport interface AnthropicStreamResult {\n  stream: ReadableStream<Uint8Array>;\n  costPromise: Promise<number>;\n}\n\n/**\n * Calls the Anthropic Messages API without streaming.\n * Used for post-processing text correction via the /post-process endpoint.\n * Returns shape matching Cerebras/Groq: { raw, usage, costUsd }\n */\nexport async function requestAnthropicChat(\n  payload: CorrectionRequestPayload,\n  requestId: string\n): Promise<{ raw: unknown; usage?: GroqUsage; costUsd: number }> {\n  const apiKey = process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY;\n  if (!apiKey) {\n    throw new Error('ANTHROPIC_API_KEY not configured');\n  }\n\n  // Convert OpenAI chat format to Anthropic format:\n  // messages[0] = system prompt, messages[1] = user message\n  const systemContent = (payload.messages[0]?.content || '')\n    + `\\n\\n${ANTHROPIC_WRAPPER_INSTRUCTION}`;\n  const userContent = payload.messages[1]?.content || '';\n\n  const response = await fetch(ANTHROPIC_API_URL, {\n    method: 'POST',\n    headers: {\n      'x-api-key': apiKey,\n      'anthropic-version': ANTHROPIC_VERSION,\n      'content-type': 'application/json',\n    },\n    body: JSON.stringify({\n      model: ANTHROPIC_MODEL,\n      max_tokens: ANTHROPIC_MAX_TOKENS,\n      system: systemContent,\n      messages: [{ role: 'user', content: userContent }],\n      stream: false,\n    }),\n  });\n\n  if (!response.ok) {\n    const errorText = await response.text().catch(() => '');\n    const error = new Error(`Anthropic API error: ${response.status} ${errorText.slice(0, 500)}`);\n    (error as any).status = response.status;\n    throw error;\n  }\n\n  const data = await response.json() as {\n    content: Array<{ type: string; text?: string }>;\n    stop_reason?: string | null;\n    usage: {\n      input_tokens: number;\n      output_tokens: number;\n      cache_creation_input_tokens?: number;\n      cache_read_input_tokens?: number;\n    };\n  };\n\n  const inputTokens = data.usage?.input_tokens || 0;\n  const outputTokens = data.usage?.output_tokens || 0;\n  const cacheCreationTokens = data.usage?.cache_creation_input_tokens || 0;\n  const cacheReadTokens = data.usage?.cache_read_input_tokens || 0;\n  const costUsd = computeAnthropicCost(inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheCreationTokens, cacheReadTokens);\n\n  console.log(`[${requestId}] Anthropic usage: input=${inputTokens}, output=${outputTokens}, cacheWrite=${cacheCreationTokens}, cacheRead=${cacheReadTokens}, cost=$${costUsd.toFixed(6)}`);\n","numLines":95,"startLine":1,"totalLines":264}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e107f99c-a035-42e7-b54a-1e23e0014aeb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Every POST /post-process 500s with \"CEREBRAS_API_KEY not configured\", even though LLM_PROVIDER_FALLBACKS.cerebras = 'groq' and GROQ_API_KEY is healthy. Same for anthropic→cerebras and groq→cerebras. A user sending X-LLM-Provider: grok in the identical state (XAI_API_KEY missing) is silently and correctly served by anthropic, because xai-llm.ts:17 sets `.status = 503`.\ntrigger_path: POST /post-process → postProcessRoute (routes/post-process.ts:140) → callWithRetry → requestCerebrasChat/requestAnthropicChat → bare `throw new Error('..._API_KEY not configured')` → catch at post-process.ts:141 → shouldFallback(error) → getErrorStatus (llm-provider.ts:162) reads `.status` = undefined, message regex `/status\\s+(\\d{3})/i` finds nothing → false → errorResponse(500) at post-process.ts:171\noracle: [A2/A3]\n```\nExact asymmetry: `xai-llm.ts:17`, `openai-llm.ts:35`, `gemini-llm.ts:19`, `mistral-llm.ts:19` all do `(error as {status?: number}).status = 503;` on the missing-key branch. `anthropic.ts:45`, `cerebras.ts:16`, `groq-llm.ts:25` do not. `hyperwhisper-cloud` has no startup env validation (`src/index.ts` does not check any provider key), so the path is reachable in production. The oracle's actual flagged line, `anthropic.ts:73`, is fine — it is the HTTP-error path and matches every sibling; only the cast style (`as any` vs the typed `as { status?: number; provider?: string }`) differs, and anthropic additionally omits the `.provider` tag, which nothing currently reads.\n\n```\nfile: /Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/src/db/index.ts\nline: 4\nsummary: PLANETSCALE_DATABASE_URL is non-null-asserted in three places but is absent from the env schema that next.config.mjs enforces at build time, so a missing value passes build/deploy and fails as an opaque TypeError inside the first DB query of a live request.\nfailure_scenario: Var unset or renamed on the deploy target. Build succeeds (serverSchema never mentions it). At runtime `drizzle(undefined, { schema })` takes the `construct(params[0], params[1])` branch of drizzle-orm/node-postgres/driver.js:74 with a client of `undefined`, so the first query throws \"Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'query')\" — every licence lookup, credit grant and admin route 500s with an error that names neither the database nor the env var.\ntrigger_path: deploy with PLANETSCALE_DATABASE_URL absent → next.config.mjs:8 imports src/env/server.mjs → serverSchema.safeParse passes (name not in schema) → any request → db-layer (e.g. findAccountByKey) → db from src/db/index.ts:4\noracle: [A2/A3]\n```\nCombined env line as requested: `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL` and `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` **are** all required in `serverSchema` (`nextjs/src/env/schema.mjs`), so `stripe.ts:13` and `redis.ts:4-5` are genuinely benign. `PLANETSCALE_DATABASE_URL` is the only one of the three asserted-but-unvalidated (also `drizzle.config.ts:7`, `src/db/migrate.ts:6`).\n\n**BENIGN**\n- `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/utils.ts:96` (`throw lastError!`) — unreachable. The loop is `attempt = 0; attempt <= maxRetries`, so it runs at least once for any `maxRetries >= 0`, and the sole caller chain is `post-process.ts` → `callWithRetry` → `retriesFor(provider)`, an exhaustive switch over the `LLMProvider` union returning only the literals 0/1/2/3 (cerebras 0, grok 1, openai 1, anthropic 2, gemini 2, mistral 2, groq 3). No caller supplies a negative or NaN count, and `retryWithBackoff` has no other call site anywhere in the repo. (`nextjs/lib/services/email.ts` has its own separate `maxRetries = 3` loop and does not use this helper.) Latent hazard only if a future caller passes a computed count.\n- `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/providers/azure-mai.ts:132` — guarded by `const isMonolingual = language && language.toLowerCase() !== 'auto'` at line 122; the `!` exists only because TS drops narrowing through the intermediate `const`. Identical guard shape to every sibling STT provider (deepgram.ts:50, elevenlabs.ts:62, openai.ts:67, mistral.ts:55, assemblyai.ts:449), so there is no fallback-path asymmetry.\n- `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/providers/google-chirp.ts:168` — same guard at line 158, same reason. No sibling asymmetry.\n- `nextjs/src/lib/license-validation.ts:141` — `importLicenseFromPolar` has exactly two `success: true` returns (lines 51 and 101) and both populate `licenseId` from a non-nullable DB primary key; every other return is `success: false` and is short-circuited at line 132 before the assertion. `findAccountById` is a typed `eq(accountKeys.id, id)` lookup, so a hypothetical undefined would match nothing rather than leak rows — but it cannot be reached.\n- `nextjs/server/api/routers/admin/customers.ts:218` and `:239` — `key` is definitely assigned: the `for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++)` loop always executes its first iteration and assigns `key = generateLicenseKey()` before any `break`/`throw`. TS's definite-assignment analysis just cannot prove a `for` body runs. The collision escape at `i === 4` throws before falling through.\n- `nextjs/src/i18n/request.ts:9` — the `as any` only widens the argument type for `readonly string[].includes()`; the runtime comparison still happens, and an unrecognised locale falls through to `routing.defaultLocale` (\"en\"). Verified `src/i18n/locales.ts` lists 40 locales and `messages/` holds exactly those 40 JSON files with no gaps in either direction, so the dynamic `import(\\`../../messages/${locale}.json\\`)` can never miss. Wrong-language service is not reachable either, since `middleware.ts` uses `localePrefix: \"always\"` with a regex built from the same `locales` array.\n- `nextjs/app/[locale]/older-versions/page.tsx:120` — guarded by `versionMap.has(buildNumber)` on the preceding line, in the same synchronous block. No miss possible.
